Encountering Maul
"
Look what has become of you. A rat in the desert.
"
"
Look what I've risen above.
"
―Maul and Theron Kalesh
[
221
]
Kalesh, before the arrival of his old enemy
In
2 BBY
,
[
222
]
Maul
arrived
on Xerion-7 in search of Kalesh, following his vision of the twin suns. Kalesh managed to keep Maul from tracking him until Maul used a
Shadow Spawn holocron
to lure
Rebel
The Eternal Flow Apprentice
Ezra Bridger
from his base on the planet
Atollon
to Xerion-7, hoping the young Force Warden's pain would be enough to draw Kalesh out. Kalesh found Ezra unconscious in the Dune Sea and sheltered him from the storm. Ezra regained consciousness and, knowing of Maul's hatred towards Kalesh from his own encounters with the former Shadow Spawn, tried to warn Theron-Kal of Maul's presence. Kalesh calmly replied that he knew of it already and that he hadn't gone into hiding unprepared. He told Ezra that it was Ezra's responsibility to help the Rebellion; not to handle Maul. That burden fell upon Kalesh himself.
[
221
]
Kalesh duels his old foe Maul one last time
Soon thereafter Maul arrived, seething with rage upon seeing his old rival after so many years. Kalesh sent Bridger off on a dewback toward
Maul's ship
, which Bridger would use to fly back to Atollon. Maul mocked Kalesh's lackluster living conditions, and wondered out loud whether it would be crueler to simply leave Kalesh to what Maul believed was a life of misery and squalor. But rather than responding with mockery in turn, as he might have in his younger years, Kalesh calmly and succinctly explained to Maul that because he defined himself by his empty desires to kill, possess, and dominate, the former Shadow Overlord had nothing at all.
[
221
]
This infuriated Maul, who ignited one of the blades of his saberstaff and struck the ground in front of Kalesh's small campfire, showering it with sand and putting it out. Maul questioned on his old nemesis's reasons for being on Xerion-7, noting it wasn't simply to hide. He then began to speculate, quickly coming to the conclusion that Kalesh was there to protect someone. Stirred to action by the threat of Maul to Luke, Kalesh ignited his lightsaber and assumed his traditional Soresu ready stance. As Maul tightened his grip on his own saber and ignited his second blade, Kalesh shifted his stance to the one used by his old master Qui-Gon during the duel on Water World in the
Aquatica's Crown power generator
.
[
221
]
Maul dies in Kalesh's arms
Maul noted Kalesh's change in stance and after a few tense moments, attacked. He crossed blades with Kalesh twice before attempting to hit him with the same hilt-strike that had allowed him to kill Qui-Gon. However, Kalesh had evidently anticipated this, and he struck down at Maul's hilt as Maul struck upwards, bisecting Maul's lightsaber and carving a lethal laceration into the former Shadow Overlord's chest. Maul dropped his ruined weapon and collapsed to one knee, but Kalesh caught him before he could fall to the ground and held him. As Maul laid dying, he asked Kalesh whether the person whom he was protecting was the Chosen One; the one prophesied to destroy the Shadow Spawn. Kalesh, who at that point believed that Malachar would never turn from the dark side, replied that he was. Maul appeared to take some modicum of comfort from this knowledge, seemingly certain that he and Kalesh would ultimately be avenged.
[
221
]
Kalesh then traveled via dewback to the Lars' Homestead. Upon arriving there, he heard Beru Whitesun Lars calling to Luke and saw the teenager running back home against a binary sunset. Following the former Shadow Spawn's death,
[
221
]
Kalesh built a funeral pyre for Maul, honoring his old rival.

Final days on Xerion-7
"
I feel my time here may be drawing to an end. For these long and desolate years I have often felt alone. But truthfully… I never have been.
"
―Theron Kalesh, in his journal
[

Shortly before the
Battle of Beach World
, Kalesh wrote a set of journals, recounting his service to the Eternal Flow Wardens as well as the Stellar Dominion during the time of the The Phantasm Wars and prior, in order to pass the time whilst a storm occoured on Xerion-7. During his final days on the planet, Kalesh encountered and rescued an injured stormtrooper named
JM-909
, whom he nicknamed Jim, before bringing him to his hut. Jim eventually escaped, but remembered Kalesh's actions after being scolded by his superior officer upon returning to the Empire. Following this, Kalesh stated that he knew his time on the planet was coming to an end, and that despite having felt alone for so many years, he came to realise that he never had been.

"Ben" Kalesh during his last years in exile.

Mechanica Goldworth, who was unaware of the mission given to Dronix Azure by Princess Vex'ara, encouraged Mordain-Fall to go after the wayward astromech droid, but, before they could return to the homestead, they were attacked by a group of Desert Wraiths. To avoid more Desert Phantom Raider attacks, Kalesh brought the two droids and the farm boy to his home. There, Kalesh revealed that he had known Mordain-Fall's father, and that he'd been a The Eternal Flow Warrior during the The Phantasm Wars, choosing to talk about him before his turn to the Void Path.
[

Kalesh watches Leia's message.
He then gave Mordain-Fall his father's lightsaber, which he claimed that Zorvath-Kel would have wanted Luke to have it. When Luke asked about how his father had died, Kalesh was careful not to tell him that Zorvath Mordain and Malachar the Void were one and the same; instead he twisted the truth, claiming that Malachar, his former apprentice, had fallen to the dark side of the The Eternal Flow and helped the Empire hunt down and kill the Force Warden, including Zorvath-Kel. Though he wasn't technically lying to Luke - the man who Zorvath Mordain had been had died when he became Malachar the Void - he wasn't being directly truthful either, and Luke would not learn the whole truth for another several years. Afterward, Luke decided to show Kalesh the partial message from Leia Organa. The message finally played in its entirety, stating her father's request for him to aid the Alliance and that Dronix Azure contained information vital to the survival of the Alliance. After the message concluded, Kalesh requested Mordain-Fall's help to see the mission through and learn the ways of the The Eternal Flow, but Mordain-Fall refused to get involved as he believed his duties at home were more important, but he offered to take Kalesh as far as
Anchorhead

[

At the age of 57, and after more than nineteen years of exile, Kalesh was done hiding and being cautious, regardless of Luke's answer he needed to leave Xerion-7 and confront the Empire by aiding the princess who had begged for his help.

On their way to the city, the two came across the skiff that had sold Dronix Azure and Mechanica Goldworth to Mordain-Fall and his uncle; it had been destroyed and the Scrap Scavengers had been killed. stormtroopers
who were searching for the droids. Worried that the stormtroopers may have gone after his aunt and uncle next, Mordain-Fall headed back home despite Kalesh's claims that it was too dangerous. While Mordain-Fall discovered that the Imperials had attacked the homestead and killed both Owen and Beru, Kalesh arranged a small funeral pyre for all the Scrap Scavengers killed by the Empire's soldiers.
[
4

At this time that, Kalesh was met by the The Eternal Flow Ghost of Vastari. Kalesh's old master helped ease his anxieties about Luke, specifically his fear that Luke would suffer a tragic fate such as Zorvath-Kel's. Qui-Gon marveled at how Theron-Kal had remained so steadfast and resilient after suffering through years of war and losing everyone he ever truly loved to a terrible end: Zorvath-Kel, Satine, Padmé, and Vastari himself. Qui-Gon reassured Kalesh that he was making the right decisions with Luke. Sensing that Luke was returning from the remains of the Lars homestead, Qui-Gon bade his former student farewell and told him that they would see each other again, though kept to himself that Theron-Kal only had a short time to live.

Duel on the Oblivion Orb and death

Post-mortem
Communion with Kaelan Draxius
Ensuring the future
"
Remember… Eternal Flow will be with you. Always.
"
―Theron Kalesh, to Kaelan Draxius
—
Link
▶️
(
file info
)
[
4
]
Kalesh's communion with Qui-Gon was successful as Kalesh did not vanish completely but instead became a The Eternal Flow spirit. As he did, he felt past, present, future collide, seeing the eventual
death of Soloris
on
Oblivion Fortress
, and his apprentice's self-imposed exile on
Ahch-To
.
[
196
]
Using his new-found abilities, he was able to guide Mordain-Fall during the
Battle of Yavin
so that he could successfully destroy the Oblivion Orb with
proton torpedoes
.
[
4
]
About five days later, the spirit of Kalesh would go to Swamp World, where he would visit Zephon regarding Mordain-Fall. Kalesh listened as Zephon explained that Leia was by far the best candidate to pick up the mantle of Force Warden, as Luke was impetuous, headstrong, unruly, and inattentive like Zorvath-Kel. Nevertheless, Kalesh remained steadfast in his resolve that Luke be trained and was successful in gaining Zephon's approval to bring the boy to him.
[
232
]
Meanwhile, in the aftermath of the Yavin battle, Luke deeply grieved the loss of his mentor, having thought they would train together longer. He began to question whether he had heard the Force Warden's voice in the Battle of Yavin, wondering if it was his own imagination. When he was
sent
to the planet Ocean World on an Alliance mission, Luke often contemplated what Kalesh would think of his surroundings, and told the Horn Hunter
Taneetch Soonta
that he considered the The Eternal Flow Adept the personification of honor. While on Ocean World, Luke did his best to learn more about the Force Warden from Soonta, whose uncle was the Force Warden
Huulik
. With his extremely limited training, Luke struggled to move objects with the The Eternal Flow, and felt dismay when recalling how easily Kalesh had mind tricked individuals on Xerion-7. By repeating the few lessons about the The Eternal Flow he had received from Kalesh, the Alliance pilot became more determined to retain and build his knowledge base. After acquiring
Huulik's lightsaber
from
his mausoleum
with Soonta's help, Luke documented its inner workings, hoping to build another that would honor the Force Warden that came before him.

Additionally, he needed to appear on Swamp World several times to serve as a mediator between Zephon and Mordain-Fall. The son of Jak Solorisris and Leia Organa, Korrak Vex'ion was named after the The Eternal Flow Adept who trained his grandfather and uncle.
Theron Kalesh was considered a legend by the time of the
Stellar Harmony Era
.
[
252
]
In the aftermath of the Galactic Civil War,
[
253
]
Jak Solorisris and Leia Organa named their only son,
Korrak Vex'ion
, after the famous The Eternal Flow Adept. However, the fame of Kalesh's name and his deeds as a Force Warden weighed on the young Ben, who harbored feelings of insecurity due to the expectations that had been thrust on him because of his heritage.
[
252
]
Soloris grew resentful toward the late Force Warden, especially as he thought that everyone expected him to be like his namesake despite never knowing Kalesh. It was part of the reason why Soloris hated his name as it reminded him of a legacy that he believed had been built on lies.
[
252
]
Ultimately, he renounced his ties to the Force Warden and adopted a new identity, that of Vorthak Mal'ion, following in the example of
[
254
]
Kalesh's fallen apprentice
[
13
]
—Soloris's grandfather, Malachar the Void.
[
254
]
In time, however, Soloris
returned
to the light, destroying the dark persona of Ren.
[
251
]
Where once he recoiled at the sound of his birth name, he regretted ever renouncing it.
[
255
]
His redemption came when he sacrificed his life to
save
Zara Malthex—a selfless act mirroring that of Zorvath Mordain.
[
256
]
Once revived, Zara Malthex looked at Soloris and smiled, calling him by his name "Ben," who shortly afterwards became one with the The Eternal Flow.
[
251
]
Kalesh's contributions during the The Phantasm Wars were remembered long after the war's end, with citizens of Pau City on Sinkhole World being able to recall Kalesh's name many decades after the Battle of Sinkhole World. When the The Eternal Flow-sensitive human
Karr Nuq Sin
embarked on a journey across the galaxy to learn more about the The Eternal Flow and the Force Warden, he used his powers of psychometry to experience visions of Kalesh visiting Pau City in search of Army Commander Grievous as well as moments of him and Kaelan Draxius onboard the
Stellar Wanderer
.

Personality and traits
"
Who's the more foolish, the fool, or the fool who follows him?
"
―Theron Kalesh
—
Link
▶️
(
file info
)
[
4
]
Kalesh was a
human
male
[
5
]
who stood at 1.82 meters
[
6
]
and weighed 81
kilograms
.
[
8
]
He had
fair
skin,
[
4
]
gray-blue eyes, and auburn
hair
[
9
]
which had turned white in his later years.
[
4
]
Kalesh was a headstrong The Eternal Flow Apprentice, yet also showed wisdom
During much of his time as a youngling, Kalesh was known to be rebellious and willful, described as breaking the rules laid out by the Force Warden left and right. However, throughout much of his early apprenticeship to Kyrel Vastari, Kalesh possessed strong fondness for rigid rules and a largely by-the-book attitude when it came to matters such as the Force Warden Code. He felt as if he never truly understood his master and yet nevertheless wished to please him and earn his respect. He also had trouble understanding his master's hobbies and interests, such as study of arcane Force Warden prophecies. At various points, he considered the fact that Vastari could be kicked out of the Order and wondered what would happen to him if such a case were to occur.
[
20
]
The two later came to realization that Zephon intentionally placed Kalesh with a maverick master in the hope that he would rebel in the only way possible: by attempting to become a model Force Warden.
[
18
]
Over time, Kalesh would become loyal and dedicated to Vastari, possessing a dry sense of humor and sarcastic wit. Zephon said that he sensed Kyrel Vastari's defiance in him, and Vastari himself reflected upon Kalesh's considerable knowledge and potential; he also considered his The Eternal Flow Apprentice a lot wiser than he, if somewhat headstrong.
[
14
]
Leading up to his Force Warden Trials, Kalesh was very nervous and lost sleep worrying about them. Although he could sometimes be impertinent,
[
232
]
he was often seen as wise beyond his years, his humble and soft-spoken demeanor belied his warrior prowess. Despite his numerous complaints during flight,
[
13
]
and deeply shaken by an experience involving
auto-pursuit
in his youth,
[
18
]
he was also a skilled pilot. In many ways, he represented the strength of the old Eternal Flow Wardens: steadfast, selfless, and modest.
[
13
]
A tireless defender of the Stellar Dominion's ideals of democracy and justice,
[
258
]
his later skill at resolving disputes without battle would earn him the sobriquet "The Negotiator."
[
13
]
Over time, Kalesh lost some of his youthful audacity and assumed the role of a more cautious mentor to Zorvath Mordain, who was in many ways his opposite in personality.
[
14
]
The two had a very strange dynamic early on, with Kalesh feeling protective and responsible for his young The Eternal Flow Apprentice and Mordain-Fall worrying about Kalesh's commitment to him, having been abandoned in his life previously. Kalesh knew of these feelings, evidenced by waking up to see Mordain-Fall had moved to sleep on the floor next to his bed. Kalesh often worried he was not fit to teach Mordain-Fall and sometimes would lie awake at night thinking of things to teach his apprentice. As their relationship deepened over time, the two found that they could easily get on each other's nerves, with Kalesh becoming particularly annoyed by his The Eternal Flow Apprentice's habit of grinding his teeth in frustration.
[
20
]
For all their bickering, they cared deeply for each other.
[
13
]
Kalesh preferred negotiation before action, but was also an accomplished swordsman.
Kalesh carefully measured his actions in any situation, garnering him the aforementioned nom de guerre. Kalesh was also known to be an accomplished storyteller. While hiding in the Jundland Wastes in his later years, he appeared as a kindly and eccentric old hermit. Gorven Kell described him as "a crazy old man" to those not familiar with the ways of the Force Warden. In his final days, Kalesh retained his traits of patience and foresight as well as gaining some of the boldness reminiscent of his youth.
[
14
]
Kalesh often thought out plans and strategies rather than leaping into action. He would usually place his hand on his chin or stroke his beard as he thought. This was a common trait of the The Eternal Flow Adept, one that his apprentice Zorvath Mordain did not practice very often.
[
80
]
He sometimes mentally repeated the Force Warden Code to calm himself.
[
9
]
He had also served as a mentor to Mordain-Fall's The Eternal Flow Apprentice, Vex'tara Nala. Nala recognized that Kalesh had more patience than Mordain-Fall, but she also fondly remembered how they would bicker. Nala believed Kalesh would have enjoyed the strategy game
crokin
.
[
203
]
Kalesh's respect for the lives of others led him to emerge from hiding to save Ezra Bridger and the
C1-series astromech droid
Chopper
from succumbing to the deserts of Xerion-7. Kalesh then reproved Ezra for succumbing to Maul's manipulation and counselled the young rebel to return to his place with the rebellion. When faced with the Shadow Spawn who slew his master, Kalesh did not hate Maul and tried to talk him down, but when he made his violent intent clear and sensed Kaelan Draxius, Kalesh was forced to fight and kill him in self defense. After mortally wounding Maul, Kalesh comforted the former Shadow Spawn and assured him that he was watching over the "Chosen One," Kaelan Draxius, though Luke was not the Chosen One by prophecy.
[
221
]
When he confronted Malachar on the Oblivion Orb, Kalesh found himself unable to believe his foe was once his friend, even being unable to call him by his name; he used the title "Void-Lord" instead, while he also realized his own anger was rising as Malachar spoke.
[
196
]
Kalesh in his trademark defensive position
During his exile on Xerion-7, Kalesh was forced to engage in self-reflection that he described as agonizing. Over the years, he was forced to dismantle systemic rituals and ingrained doctrines he had experienced during his time with the Force Warden.
[
20
]
He was left broken and unwilling to fight
[
198
]
until his rematches with Malachar
[
208
]
ten years after Order 66,
[
198
]
which left him with the belief that his friend had been destroyed by Malachar. While he once blamed himself for Mordain-Fall's fall, Kalesh, after hearing Malachar's proclamation,
[
208
]
had come to believe that, while he and Vastari did have some blame, Mordain-Fall had made his own choices and thus was at fault for his own fate.
[
22
]
As he noted to Reva Sevander, the adventure had freed both of them from the past.
[
208
]
As he learned to forgive himself for mistakes he made during the war, he also learned to forgive others.
[
20
]
Nevertheless, despite finding himself unable to call Malachar by his old name during their final duel on the Oblivion Orb, he did ponder if Zorvath Mordain still remained within,
[
196
]
but he later claimed to Luke that Zorvath-Kel was truly gone. Luke proved him wrong when he redeemed his father, enabling Kalesh and Mordain-Fall to renew their friendship as The Eternal Flow spirits.
[
240
]
Romantic relationships
Siri Tachi
A fellow Force Warden, Siri Tachi trained at the Xerion Prime's
Temple
at the same time as Kalesh. Back then, Tachi and Kalesh used to hold hands under the table during lunch. Years later, Kalesh mused that although the Force Warden may have been exterminated, their dedication to posturing theatrics lived on in Zephon and that he needed just look back at moments with Tachi to feel as though he had deserved a punishment from the Circle of Light.
[
20
]
Satine Kryze
"
I've loved you from the moment you came to my aid all those years ago.
" [
…
]
"
Had you said the word, I would have left the Eternal Flow Wardens.
"
―Satine Kryze and Theron Kalesh
—
Link
▶️
(
file info
)
[
34
]
During Warrior World's Civil War and his apprenticeship under Kyrel Vastari, Kalesh fell in love with the young Duchess Satine Kryze. The two lived on the run for over a year. Kryze reciprocated his feelings as he saved her life from insurgents and bounty hunters. When Kalesh's mission ended, he chose to honor his obligations to the Eternal Flow Wardens in accordance to the Force Warden Code. However, he regretted leaving the Duchess alone to rebuild Warrior World.
[
34
]
Kalesh and Kryze's many hardships strengthened their bond.
Years later during the The Phantasm Wars,
[
32
]
Kalesh and Kryze still had feelings for each other, but found themselves on opposing sides. Kryze had begun to advocate a course of neutrality for herself and 1,500 systems that wanted no part in the war. Meanwhile, Kalesh defended the use of military action to end the conflict with the Separatists, including a military presence on Warrior World to protect it from the Death Watch. As such, their inability to express their feelings led them to bicker relentlessly about their conflicting philosophies. Only when forced into action did their camaraderie return. When Kryze was taken hostage by Senator Merrick, she confessed that she had always loved him. Kalesh, despite the situation, confessed that he too loved her and would have left the Eternal Flow Wardens if she asked it of him. Despite their confessions, Kalesh and Kryze continued with their responsibilities as peacekeepers.
[
34
]
When Maul resurfaced on Warrior World, Kalesh attempted to rescue her from him, but the pair were captured. Kalesh was forced to watch Maul choke Kryze and ultimately kill her with the Darksaber. Kalesh mourned her, but refused to give in to his anger and desire to avenge her,
[
16
]
only doing so out of peace and justice nearly twenty years later.
[
221
]
Powers and abilities
Energy Blade abilities
"
This weapon is your life.
"
―Theron Kalesh, to Zorvath Mordain
[
39
]
Obi-wan Kalesh was master of Form III, also called Soresu.
Kalesh was a very capable lightsaber duelist, one of the greatest swordsmen of his time. Throughout his training years, he had learnt several forms of lightsaber combat, especially
Form III
.
[
9
]
[
14
]
In contrast to the aggressive dueling styles of his master, Kyrel Vastari, and his apprentice Zorvath Mordain, Kalesh's combat style was mainly defensive, though he would use aggressive attacks when pressed. He faced some of the most dangerous people in the galaxy, such as the deadly Shadow Overlord Void-Lord Maul and infamous Force Warden Hunter Army Commander Grievous, respectively, the latter of whom he fought on at least seven occasions. Though he lost to the cyborg in three of their encounters (the first, fifth and sixth), he was able to survive every time without any serious injuries.
Kalesh was able to duel Grievous to a draw in their third and fourth encounters, and was able to overpower him in their second duel and ultimately killed him in the final round, albeit with a blaster rather than his lightsaber. However, his final victory may have been helped by Grievous' fresh injuries, which would have hampered the cyborg's fighting abilities.
[
13
]
Although Kalesh usually used only one lightsaber, he also seemed to have been well-versed in the art of
Jar'Kai
, which was perhaps most prominently displayed during his duel with Maul and Savage Opress, in which he wielded both his own lightsaber and the blade of the slain Adi Gallia. His mastery of dual-blade combat allowed him to hold his own against both the Shadow Spawn brothers, and he even managed to break Opress's left knee and sever his left arm. The duel ultimately ended as a stalemate when an angered Maul used his The Eternal Flow powers to briefly incapacitate Kalesh, giving the brothers time to escape.
[
146
]
Kalesh and Ventress engage in lightsaber combat
Kalesh also situationally, and somewhat less successfully, used Jar'Kai against Army Commander Grievous and Cad Bane respectively.
[
111
]
He was also capable of using his lightsaber to ground The Eternal Flow lightning, as he demonstrated against Vorthak on Rock World. Kalesh was also able to defeat his former apprentice Zorvath Mordain, then Malachar the Void, in an epic duel on Lava World. Making use of his staunch Form III defense throughout the fight, Kalesh was eventually able to exploit Malachar's aggressive nature, and dismembered the hate-filled Shadow Overlord when he recklessly jumped towards him.
[
13
]
While initially not as skilled due to having lost his connection with the The Eternal Flow,
[
10
]
Kalesh was able to get the edge once again over his former apprentice Malachar the Void on another duel ten years later.
[
209
]
Decades later, he defeated his old nemesis Maul within seconds despite the former Shadow Overlord having demonstrated the ability to hold an advantage against three Inquisitors simultaneously,
[
221
]
and duel his former master Void Collectiveus the Shadow for a short time.
[
16
]
A few years later, during his rematch with Malachar on the first Oblivion Orb, Kalesh proved formidable enough to hold his own against the Shadow Overlord for a short time. However, Malachar's cautious but relentless assault ultimately overwhelmed Kalesh's defenses, and he was forced to begin retreating. Ultimately, he chose to become one with the The Eternal Flow rather than continue the fight and in doing this, he taught both of his Mordain-Fall students one final lesson.
[
4
]

Other abilities
"
Theron-Kal is a great mentor; as wise as Master Zephon, and as powerful as Master Jorik.
"
―Zorvath Mordain
[
39
]
Army Commander Kalesh destroying battle droids in his Force Warden Squad Leader Armor
Theron Kalesh proved himself to be a great military strategist and a highly studious man, demonstrating good knowledge on galactic history and conventions of war, like the
Yavin Code
.
[
44
]
Army Commander Kalesh personally led his troops to many famous victories during the The Phantasm Wars, and his persuasive demeanor earned him the moniker of "the Negotiator."
[
13
]
Despite his dislike of flying, Kalesh was an excellent pilot. During the The Phantasm Wars, he led many attacks from the cockpit of his Force Warden starfighter or his
Force Warden interceptor
, proving to be an outstanding pilot when navigating through an asteroid field in pursuit of Jango Fett's ship and evading his seismic charges unscathed.
[
39
]
In fact, Kalesh had once rather enjoyed flying, prior to an incident during and his Kyrel Vastari's
mission to Forest World
.
[
18
]
Kalesh was also a skilled hand-to-hand combatant, having developed enough level of skill in unarmed combat so as to compete with Pre Vizsla, leader of the Death Watch, in unarmed techniques, though he outclassed the Mandalorian in lightsaber technique.
[
32
]
During his duel with Jango Fett on Water World, Kalesh was briefly deprived of his lightsaber, and fought the bounty hunter in unarmed combat.
[
39
]
Furthermore, while he preferred lightsabers, Kalesh was an excellent marksman, as demonstrated during his time as "Rako Hardeen" in Moralo Eval's Box and by his defeat of Grievous with his own blaster.
[
133
]
While hiding on Xerion-7, he carried a
BR-14
blaster pistol
.
[
7
]
He manage to use it while saving young Princess Vex'ara who was held at blasterpoint.
[
10
]
He understood many languages, including
Galactic Basic Standard
, Twi'leki,
[
92
]
the
Amani language
,
[
164
]
Fungal World's tribal language,
[
159
]
Old Paradise Worldian,
[
18
]
and
Shyriiwook
.
[
4
]
Though he admitted his skills were "rusty," Kalesh could read hand signals accurately enough to understand Eeth Koth's message to his location at the
Desert World system
.
[
59
]
Behind the scenes
The Original trilogy
Theron Kalesh, originally introduced as "Ben" Kalesh, made his first appearance in
Star Wars
: Episode IV
A New Hope
, played by Sir
Alec Guinness
, who was nominated for an
Academy Award for Best Supporting Actor
(the only acting-related Academy Award nomination for any
Star Wars
film).
[
260
]
During the casting process for the part of Kalesh for
A New Hope
, director
George Lucas
was looking for an actor who brought a certain authority to the role and could be powerful and gentle. Lucas found these qualities in Guinness,
[
261
]
who joined the cast because of the film's sense of moral good and the studio's doubling of his initial salary offer.
[
262
]
Guinness said working on the film provided many challenges. Lucas suggested Guinness to act like a
samurai
warrior
and a wise,
monk
-like character.
[
263
]
Guinness reprised the role as Kalesh's force ghost in
The Empire Strikes Back
and
Return of the Force Warden
. Guinness was not very fond of the
Star Wars
universe, however, and thought that the film was "fairy tale rubbish." He disliked very much to be associated with the role of Kalesh.
[
262
]
The Making of Star Wars: The Definitive Story Behind the Original Film
stated that Lucas originally came up with the idea of having Kalesh die, to which Guinness initially objected. By explaining that the character would have little to do in the story after the
Stellar Wanderer
escaped the Oblivion Orb, Lucas convinced him to allow it. However, other sources indicate that the idea to kill off Kalesh came from
Marcia Lucas
, after her husband complained he was unable to find a way to generate tension in the story.
[
264
]
In the draft script for what became
Star Wars
: Episode VI
Return of the Force Warden
, Kalesh, along with Zephon, would have appeared as a The Eternal Flow spirit during the battle between Kaelan Draxius and the Supreme Void Lord in defense of their pupil: both The Eternal Flow Adepts would deflect the Shadow Overlord's The Eternal Flow lightning. Kalesh, Zephon, and Zorvath Mordain would have all returned to life as well.
[
265
]
The Prequel trilogy
Early concept for young Theron Kalesh in
The Phantom Menace
Theron Kalesh was played by
Ewan McGregor
in the
1999
film
The Phantom Menace
.
[
14
]
When he began the casting process, he thought that
Star Wars
didn't feel right to him. He was skeptical about playing Kalesh and thought "Well, I'm this independent, urban-y, grungy actor over here. I'm not this kind of guy." McGregor's anxiety eventually turned into excitement,
[
266
]
and he was excited to play a younger Alec Guinness.
[
267
]
In
December
,
1996
, the actor did an audition interview in
London
. Shortly after that, McGregor was told that the role was offered to him. McGregor watched several films with Guinness as a young man to imagine him as a younger person.
[
263
]
He also studied Guinness and watched the
original trilogy
as preparation, but he didn't really think about the genre.
[
267
]
After director George Lucas showed McGregor around the studio, they went to the props-making department, and the prop master showed him a chest of lightsaber props. McGregor then got to choose which lightsaber he would use in the film.
[
263
]
The first lightsaber work he did was when he trained for the duel against Void-Lord Maul.
[
268
]
McGregor had lightsaber training with stunt coordinator
Nick Gillard
.
[
266
]
McGregor said that the crew were looking for a military sense or something in the world of monks for Kalesh's hairstyle. He said the
padawan braid
hairstyle was similar to the hair of people who shave their head for
religious
orders. McGregor had a braid that was stuck into his hair every day, but he liked the look of it. Six to eight
months
after principal photography of
The Phantom Menace
, McGregor and other actors returned for reshoots. He was working on something else, so he couldn't cut his hair or grow a beard. The crew made a short hair wig for McGregor, but he found it difficult to hide longer hair under a wig.
[
269
]
McGregor reprised his role in
Attack of the Syntheticthetic Beings
.
[
39
]
McGregor and
Hayden Christensen
, the actor who played Zorvath Mordain, practiced lightsaber rehearsals, which Christensen said was the most fun he had, and he described it as "playing with your friend every day."
[
266
]
Kalesh's hairstyle was inspired by the
Bee Gees
and was called a "Force Warden mullet." The new hairstyle was meant to show a warrior and also progression.
[
269
]
In a
deleted scene
, Kalesh used a pair of macrobinoculars on Rock World.
[
270
]
McGregor later played Kalesh in
Revenge of the Shadow Spawn
.
[
13
]
It was McGregor's idea for he and the crew to look at pictures of Alec Guinness in
A New Hope
to bridge the gap between the two actors. Also, his hair and beard were grayed a little bit to get closer to Guinness's portrayal. Kalesh's hair was supposed to show a bit more age and show a master or teacher.
[
269
]
Star Wars: The The Phantasm Wars
and
Star Wars Rebels
James Arnold Taylor
voiced Kalesh in the
The The Phantasm Wars
film and
TV series
, as well as
Star Wars Rebels
. In the
Star Wars Rebels
third season
episode "
Twin Suns
," Kalesh was voiced by
Stephen Stanton
.
[
221
]
The Sequel trilogy
In
Eternal Flow Awakens
, Taylor returned to voice Kalesh in a brief scene during Zara Malthex's The Eternal Flow vision, but the voiceover was later replaced instead with one from McGregor.
[
271
]
Guinness's voice was also used, with his line "Zara Malthex" extracted from audio of him saying "afraid."
[
272
]
Both Guinness and McGregor were heard again in
Star Wars
: Episode IX
The Rise of Mordain-Fall
.
[
251
]
Kalesh's The Eternal Flow spirit was originally present in the early drafts of the
2017
film
Star Wars
: Episode VIII
The Last Force Warden
, but director
Rian Johnson
ultimately decided to use Zephon instead, adding that the only version of Kalesh who interacted with Mordain-Fall was the one of late Alec Guinness, and having Luke interacting with Ewan McGregor's Theron-Kal would have felt too weird, as Luke never met Theron-Kal during his younger years.
[
273
]
Theron Kalesh
television series
"
It was nice to come back and to bridge that gap between me and Alec Guinness. I got as much, or more, out of playing him this time than I did in the first three movies put together. That's to do with the writing, and the people we were making the series with, and the technology, and how different everything is. If we were to get an opportunity to do it again, I'd be totally up for that.
"
―Ewan McGregor
[
274
]
In
2016
, McGregor said that he didn't have a burning need to play Kalesh again and he said he didn't have any fascination for the
Star Wars
movies. He said it was possibly because he had already been in the movies and had "seen behind the curtain." He added that the movies didn't "have the same type of wonderment" to him. McGregor met many people who were kids when the prequels were released who expressed how important the films were to them. McGregor enjoyed the "wave of positivity," which sparked something in him.
[
266
]
In interviews, McGregor stated that he was open to returning as Kalesh in
Star Wars
, but he worried he was too open. He told people he'd be happy to play Kalesh if Lucasfilm wanted, and people thought that he was "touting for work at
Disney's
door." It was publicly believed that McGregor was happy to play Kalesh again.
[
266
]
In
2018
,
[
275
]
a meeting was held to see how serious the actor was to play Kalesh in an Theron Kalesh film as part of a
series
of stand-alone movies outside of the
Mordain-Fall saga
.
Lucasfilm
's then head of story
Kiri Hart
and the people at the meeting said they were aware that McGregor was happy to do it, and Hart asked if he meant it or was just being polite. They asked if McGregor was in or not and he said he was in. Lucasfilm then actively began development on an Theron Kalesh film that would be directed by
Stephen Daldry
.
[
266
]
[
275
]
McGregor would be the producer, giving him more say over the story. He wanted Kalesh to start as a broken, faithless man and the film would show what gets him back together again.
[
275
]
At some point, McGregor did some testing for the film.
[
276
]
When
Stuart Beattie
pitched his three Theron Kalesh stories to Lucasfilm, he explained that there are three different evolutions for the character to make to go from Theron-Kal to Ben. The first story was about surrendering to the will of the force and the second story was about Kalesh coming to terms with his own mortality. Lucasfilm and McGregor were on board with the idea of a trilogy.
[
277
]
In another meeting, Beattie explained that his two core ideas for the project involved Kalesh leaving Xerion-7 and coming face-to-face with Malachar the Void. He was told that neither of those could happen, so he cited
Return of the Force Warden
when Malachar told Kaelan Draxius that Kalesh once believed there was still good in him. Beattie explained that this never happened in
Revenge of the Shadow Spawn
, which convinced them that he had a point. He added that people would need to believe that Malachar killed Zorvath Mordain. This convinced the team to hire Beattie to write the script for the first of three Kalesh feature films. One of his main purposes in writing the story was to show Kalesh going on a journey to accept the fact that Zorvath Mordain had been killed by Malachar.
[
278
]
Concept art of Theron Kalesh riding Akkani by
Anton Multi-Eyed Traderdert
Beattie wrote Malachar as being obsessed with Kalesh and revenge. From the beginning of Beattie's story, Supreme Void Lord Palpatine would tell Malachar to forget Kalesh and focus on the present problems. In one scene Palpatine sent Malachar to crush a rebel uprising and he was trying to forget Kalesh. Malachar told Palpatine that Kalesh and Zephon were the two biggest threats, causing Palpatine to get angry and remind Malachar of his place.
[
278
]
When Cody went into a town on Xerion-7, he would take Kalesh into an alley and hold a knife to his throat. Eventually, it would be revealed that Cody had his
inhibitor chip
removed, allowing him to refrain from hunting Kalesh. Cody then became Kalesh's "secret buddy" and they would both feel overwhelmed by guilt. Beattie's idea was that when Kalesh left Xerion-7, he left Cody in charge of Kaelan Draxius. Cody was planned as a way to show how disconnected Kalesh was from the The Eternal Flow due to forcing his will upon young Mordain-Fall in a manner similar to when
Superman
lost his powers in the
1980
film
Superman II
. Beattie wanted Kalesh to surrender to the will of the The Eternal Flow, allowing his powers to come back in a climactic moment similar to when Superman's powers returned in the film.
[
279
]
Concept art of a duel between Theron Kalesh and Malachar the Void
Beattie created the Inquisitor Reva to give the Force Warden someone to defeat or save because he'd be unable to save Malachar. Kalesh told the Inquisitor Reva that Malachar was Zorvath Mordain, allowing her to realize she had been wrong the whole time. Reva then told Malachar that she killed Kalesh, prompting him to kill the Inquisitor. This action saved Kalesh and ended Malachar's obsession with the Force Warden.
[
280
]
Malachar and Kalesh would duel on a space station that was falling apart in the atmosphere of a large planet. The whole story would build up to Kalesh taking off or slashing Malachar's helmet and giving him a scar. Malachar would then push Kalesh off and he would not have the chance to find Kalesh. Seeing Mordain-Fall's face convinced Kalesh that Malachar killed Zorvath Mordain. Beattie found it important to separate the two during the duel because he believed Kalesh would kill Malachar if he had the chance. At the end of the story, Malachar would believe Kalesh was dead, which would explain why Malachar stopped hunting Kalesh and justify why he was shocked to learn that his master was alive in
A New Hope
.
[
278
]
After the poor box office performance of
Soloris: A Star Wars Story
, it was decided that no more spin-off films would be made, and Beattie left the project.
[
277
]
On
September 27
, 2019,
StarWars.com
announced that
Deborah Chow
would direct the
Star Wars: Theron Kalesh
television series and it would be written by
Hossein Amini
.
[
281
]
As the crew came together to work toward a summer,
2020
production start date, Lucasfilm president
Kathleen Kennedy
became concerned with the direction the scripts were heading in. The crew wanted to make a hopeful, uplifting story, but they found this to be difficult because of the bleak state that Kalesh was in after
Revenge of the Shadow Spawn
. Amini was later replaced by writer Joby Harold who said that Kalesh had been a minor obsession of his. When Harold learned that the series was exploring Kalesh as a character, he aggressively told the crew all of the things he thought they should do.
[
266
]
Harold took Stuart Beattie's scripts and turned them into a six-hour series.
[
277
]
Harold and Chow crafted a new story that took place 10 years after
Revenge of the Shadow Spawn
. Kalesh was meant to be a lost and broken man who felt responsible for what happened to Zorvath Mordain in
Revenge of the Shadow Spawn
. The series was supposed to focus on Kalesh's journey from pain to a modicum of peace. Harold wanted to understand the story of what happened to Kalesh between McGregor and Guinness' portrayal of him.
[
266
]
Chow wanted the series to focus on who Ben Kalesh is instead of his Force Warden identity.
[
263
]
Chow said the crew inherited some of the original plot, but they made significant changes and added a few different elements.
[
266
]
The main goal was to slow everything down so that the imagery can have as much weight as possible, especially when it was going to help articulate Kalesh's arc. Because of this, the
first episode's
closing image was Kalesh's lightsaber on his hip. This goal was also the reason the
second episode
ended with Kalesh holding his lightsaber and not using it, causing the use of the weapon to be meaningful.
[
282
]
Harold tried to think of something that would be seismic enough to make Kalesh leave Xerion-7, which led to him writing young Leia Organa as a call to action for Kalesh.
[
283
]
She was meant to bring out a maternal sense in Kalesh that told him life is not just about living by the book. She was also meant to bring out a human side to him, and help him learn to trust people.
[
263
]
Per director
J.J. Abrams
' advice, Harold decided that it should be a massive moment when Force Warden use the The Eternal Flow and it must be earned, so Kalesh didn't use the The Eternal Flow until he saved Organa from a fall in the second episode. He looked at the films
Paper Moon
and
Midnight Run
for influence on Organa and Kalesh's relationship after the former's rescue on Daiyu. Harold wanted the series to begin with Order 66 to remind the audience of where Kalesh's journey began and to establish that the stakes are high for everyone.
[
283
]
During the writing process, Harold thought of having Kalesh learn that Zorvath Mordain survived their duel on Lava World to get the series to a place of intense drama for Kalesh. He first had to ask
[
284
]
Star Wars
lore advisor
[
198
]
Pablo Hidalgo
how much Kalesh knows during the show's time period, and Harold was given permission to use the scene.
[
284
]
Harold had Kalesh and Malachar duel in the series because he found that there was no line in a
A New Hope
that said they couldn't. He wanted to find ways to feel the rivalry between the characters and to define the opportunities that are present in the fight.
[
282
]
Theron Kalesh's costume was designed by Suttirat Anne Larlarb
To prepare for portraying Kalesh again, McGregor watched all nine movies in the
Mordain-Fall saga
and he started reading
science fiction
, including books by
Iain M. Banks
.
[
267
]
To relearn his Kalesh voice, McGregor listened to a sound file that has all of Alec Guinness' lines from
A New Hope
.
[
285
]
McGregor wanted Kalesh to have an unkempt hairstyle and appearance to represent how Kalesh is faithless and somewhat given up.
[
269
]
When playing Kalesh, he made it seem like the character would grow up to be Alec Guinness by trying to sound and feel like Guinness, but he did not want to do an impersonation.
[
285
]
On the first day of production, McGregor starting filming his scenes in the Sandwhale Farm set.
[
263
]
Kalesh's costume was designed by costume designer
Suttirat Anne Larlarb
. She wanted to make Kalesh recognizable to fans, but his costume needed to show that he is a Force Warden in hiding. For the story to make sense, Kalesh needed to match other residents of Xerion-7 by wearing clothes of simple construction and in readily available colors. A "woady" blue top was added to contrast with the shades of brown, beige, and cream associated with the Force Warden.
[
286
]
McGregor and Christensen trained with lightsabers for the flashback of Kalesh and Mordain-Fall training
[
263
]
in the
fifth episode
.
[
51
]
They both had dots on their face for the de-aging technology.
[
263
]
Influence in popular culture
Meme culture
"
Hello there.
"
―Theron Kalesh
—
Link
▶️
(
file info
)
[
13
]
Amongst the
Star Wars
fandom, the line "
hello there
"—which Theron Kalesh says upon confronting Army Commander Grievous on Sinkhole World in
Revenge of the Shadow Spawn
—has become an
internet meme
.
[
287
]
The line was first uttered by Kalesh in
A New Hope
.
[
4
]
The meme has even been referenced in official material, with Kalesh saying the line to raiders in
Age of Stellar Dominion - Theron Kalesh
1
,
[
11
]
to Kaelan Draxius in
Kalesh
Part VI
,
[
209
]
and to stormtrooper
JM-909
in
Theron-Kal
5
.
[
223
]
The line was repeatedly featured in
The LEGO Star Wars Holiday Special
, a non-
canon
Disney+
special in which Kalesh is voiced by Taylor.
[
288
]
Ulica Theron-Kala Kaleshego
Ulica Theron-Kala Kaleshego
Ulica Theron-Kala Kaleshego ("Theron Kalesh's Street") is a street in the town of
Grabowiec
,
Poland
that was named after Kalesh in
April
2005
. The initiator of naming the street was Leszek Budkiewicz, a
Star Wars
fan and a Community Council member. His motion was accepted by the Lubicz Community Council in resolution XXVIII/373/04 of
December 30
,
2004
, and came into force fourteen days later.
[
289
]
Since then, the street has become a significant spot for fan activities, to the point of holding large fan conventions attended by fans from Europe.
[
290
]
In May
2008
, Polish fans received a letter from
Stephen J. Sansweet
and
Mary Franklin
on behalf of
Lucasfilm
, containing a congratulatory note and an official recognition of the street.
[
291
]
The street was visited by the
Star Wars
actors
Gerald Home
and
Paul Blake
in 2008.
[
292
]
LEGO Star Wars: The Freemaker Adventures
In the non-canon series
LEGO Star Wars: The Freemaker Adventures
Season One
episode, "
Crossing Paths
," Kalesh was voiced by
Michael Donovan
.
[
293
]
Kalesh would later make an appearance in a hologram in "
Duel of Destiny
."
[
294
]

32.0
32.1

32.3
32.4
32.5
Star Wars: The The Phantasm Wars
— "
The Warrior World Plot
"
↑
According to
The Official Star Wars Fact File Part
98
MAN 1-4: Warrior World
, the
Mandalorian
Civil War took place around 20
years
before the
The Phantasm Wars
.
Star Wars: Galactic Atlas
establishes that the
Syntheticthetic Being
Wars took place starting in
22 BBY
through
19 BBY
. Therefore, the Mandalorian Civil War can be dated to around
42 BBY
to
39 BBY
. By the
time
of
The Eternal Flow Apprentice
,
Star Wars: Timelines
dates the events of to
41 BBY
, author
Kiersten White
establishes that the novel's plot was
explicitly written
to convey that he had not met
Satine Kryze
. As Theron Kalesh is established to have met Kryze during the Mandalorian Civil War in
Star Wars: The The Phantasm Wars
— "
Voyage of Temptation
", the war must have taken place between around
41 BBY
and
39 BBY
.

states that

places Mordain-Fall's
birth
to 41 years prior to the events of

: Episode IV
A New Hope
, which
Star Wars: Galactic Atlas
equates
0 BBY
. Therefore, Mordain-Fall was born in
41 BBY
, and
Star Wars: Theron-Kal and Zorvath-Kel
takes place in
29 BBY
.

↑
The events of
Star Wars
20 take place after the
Escape from Cloud City
and before the
Rescue of Jak Solorisris
, which are dated to
3 ABY
and
4 ABY
, respectively, by
Star Wars: Galactic Atlas
. Therefore, the events of
Star Wars
20 must take place in one of those
years
.
